ParlaMint-SI_2004-02-25-SDZ3-Redna-34.ana.u116	Thank you very much, Mr. President. Persons with disabilities are a category of persons who certainly need more attention and protection in all aspects of social inclusion, including in employment. In the past, however, protection was often too large, often pushing people with disabilities to the brink. These have been exempted from all social settings and left to domestic supplies or their intended institutions. Therefore, it is not surprising to learn that the majority of people with disabilities are less educated, unemployed, and forcibly retired. The law that talks about their employment has been 27 years old and has not changed in all these years, which in itself says the necessity of adopting a new law, a modern one, which will allow people with disabilities an equal opportunity of employment compared to others. The work and thus the possibility of surviving yourself and your family is a constitutionally defined category which must be made possible for all citizens, whether they are disabled or not. That is why we are so much more pleased that the government has drawn up a proposal for a law that will at least ease the deprived position of persons with disabilities in the employment market if it is not possible to remedy it altogether. Unfortunately, there are a number of provisions in the bill that we are not satisfied with. We believe that this cannot be the law under which the status of a disabled person will be granted. The status of a disabled person should be ensured under a system-wide law which should specify exactly who is a disabled person, what levels and what conditions are there for obtaining this status. Thus, disability status can be acquired on the basis of different laws, acts and regulations. In the proposal for a law, even a provision which states that the Minister responsible for disability shall issue a regulation specifying the content of the criteria for the recognition of the status of disabled person in this law. This is how we adopt a law that we don't even know who it's going to be, because it's probably not even ready yet. This increases the possibility of non-compliance, as will the disabled. Even now, problems arise when persons with disabilities are granted the status of disabled persons under the Social Protection Act of mentally and physically disabled persons who cannot be employed by a disabled person. To achieve this, it must first change the status of a disabled worker. However, there is no provision for a change of status since the procedures for this process are neither defined nor prescribed. While this can be carried out in practice, these procedures are long-lasting. Thus, the rights under the Social Protection Act stop mentally and physically affected persons as soon as you apply for the status of a disabled worker, but you cannot get a job until you get a change of status, which may also take years. It is essential that this law also regulate the transition from the so-called non-working to the disabled. The bill also introduces a new concept, namely quotas. The Quota Employment Scheme will mean that companies will be forced to employ disabled persons within a certain percentage of the total number of employees, but it is unclear what these quotas will be, as their level will be determined by the Government on a proposal from the Economic and Social Council by the end of this year. So we are once again adopting provisions that we do not know what they will be or whether these quotas can change if the situation of employment of persons with disabilities changes or not. Obviously, it is already customary for the government to adopt laws without clear provisions, since the definition of the under-laws is certainly easier than changes to the law. The quota system sounds good, but maybe it's not very thoughtful. I wonder if any analysis, perhaps consequences, has been done to introduce quotas into Slovenian companies? Many countries that have introduced quotas are now gradually abandoning them because they have not produced the desired effects. One such country is the United Kingdom, which is now facing anti-discrimination legislation with the unemployment of persons with disabilities. Forcing the employment of disabled persons only due to the provisions of the law is certainly not going to achieve its purpose. Companies should see the benefit and contribution of the company in the employment of persons with disabilities. Tax incentives would certainly be welcome, and the Law on Taxation of Legal Persons would abolish them. We ask ourselves why companies would seek employment of disabled people if they did not have any direct benefits. Disability as workers is particularly protected and cannot be easily forgiven, which on the one hand seems to be social justice and humane and, on the other hand, disstimulates the company for their employment. So why should businesses hire disabled people if they do not benefit from this before the restrictions? So with this disabled person, we don't do any good looking for jobs, rather the other way around. For the employer, employment of disabled persons will mainly be a restriction and not an advantage. Persons with disabilities will constitute an unforgivable workforce and no disability companies will receive tax credits. On the other hand, last year we passed a law on working relationships where disabled persons as workers are not specifically mentioned. The bill also lacks incentives for self-employment of people with disabilities, which could be a contribution to start-up stress or otherwise defined. Self-employment is one of the incentive activities to reduce unemployment, and we believe that this activity should be further stimulated in the case of people with disabilities. In preparing the law, the government did not invite employers' representatives to participate, so it did not give its opinion to the Chamber of Commerce or any other association, a company which could represent the interests of employers. And there are some other things that have interfered with our reading of this bill. In preparing this proposal, the Government has taken into account the opinion of the Economic and Social Council and has not requested the opinion of the Council of the Republic of Slovenia on persons with disabilities, the purpose of which is to give opinions in the drafting of disability legislation. We're wondering why that is. In the Youth Party, we will support the proposed law, but we expect the proposer to examine our comments and include them in the draft law. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-SI_2004-02-25-SDZ3-Redna-34.ana.u117	Thank you very much, Comrade Levich. The position of the Parliamentary Club of Liberal Democracy of Slovenia will now be presented by colleague Ljubo Germič, behind him by colleague Lavrič. First, Comrade Germich, please.
ParlaMint-SI_2004-02-25-SDZ3-Redna-34.ana.u118	Dear Mr. President! Mr. Minister, ladies and gentlemen of the Ministry, distinguished colleagues, distinguished colleagues! The proposal for the Law on Employment Rehabilitation and Employment of Persons with Disabilities, which is before us today, is a law that we have long anticipated and that, together with the laws already adopted in this area, largely concludes the issue under consideration. In the Liberal Democracy Club of Slovenia, we have carefully examined the bill and assessed that it brings a number of new solutions and comparisons with existing legislation, and in our view it will certainly help solve the problem of employment of people with disabilities. However, we expect certain corrections and additions based on today's debate. Article 52 of the Constitution of the Republic of Slovenia provides that persons with disabilities are provided with protection and training for work in accordance with the law. The employment of persons with disabilities in the Republic of Slovenia is currently governed by laws which some stem from a period prior to independence. The Law on the Training and Employment of Persons with Disabilities, 1976, which is a fundamental law for this area, is long overdue and has been fully preserved due to significant changes in the economic and social system. Today's picture in the field of employment of persons with disabilities shows that the unemployment rates of persons with disabilities are higher than the general unemployment rate in the country, particularly for the disabled, for the disabled over 50 years and for the disabled who have a low level of education. In addition, a more accurate view of the structure of disabled persons shows that the proportions of disabled persons in these segments are the highest. In Slovenia, there are currently more than 11,000 unemployed people with disabilities, so the proportion of people with disabilities among the unemployed is 11.5%. In international regulation, the situation of persons with disabilities is governed by a number of United Nations acts, in particular the 1975 Declaration on the Rights of Persons with Disabilities and the 1993 Standard Rules for the Equalization of the Opportunities of Persons with Disabilities, which impose individual activities and procedures on countries and social partners to create equal opportunities for persons with disabilities. Following the consideration of proposals to amend the Constitution of the Republic of Slovenia, we followed these documents in the LDS House of Members and supported the proposal of a group of Members to amend Article 14 of the Constitution, which should also include disability in the article on equality before the law. We have always had before us the fact that this is not just about regulating the rights of persons with disabilities, but about the application of the complete civilisation principle of non-discrimination. This is also how we see the proposal for a law that is before us today, because we believe that it is absolutely necessary because the existing legislation is obsolete because the position of the disabled in the labour market is less favourable because the disabled are less involved in the labour market because their unemployment rate is higher and because the duration of their unemployment is longer than that of the non-disabled population. Finally, we are bound by European legislation, the Council of the European Union Directive 2000/78, the resolution of equal employment opportunities for people with disabilities and the European Employment Strategy, which is in the process of being adopted. In the Liberal Democracy Club, we believe that the objectives of the law are mainly to increase the employment rate and increase the employability of people with disabilities in Slovenia, to increase the social inclusion of persons with disabilities and to ensure the opportunity for equal participation of persons with disabilities in the labour market by creating adequate jobs and working conditions for persons with disabilities and the principle of their realization, adequately represented and reflected in the law their definition and means of implementation. Above all, in the LDS Club, it seems important to us that the bill highlights the movement from passive to active measures: that the bill introduces a social, functional model for dealing with disability, which is not based on disability, but on the ability of persons with disabilities to engage in work, to work and active life in society; to allow access to work, to provide equal opportunities to acquire skills for the entry market and to maintain employment, including the use of information and communication technologies and the transition to knowledge-based society; to focus active and preventive measures on appropriate employment opportunities, to raise awareness of employers and to new opportunities in the social economy. The solutions put forward by the law are generally acceptable to the LDS Club as they are aimed at eliminating the social exclusion of people with disabilities and their involvement in employment by developing their skills. Finally, the proposal for a law also provides an economic justification for rehabilitation, since it is also a positive item in the budget of a certain company that is successfully implementing it. If the cost benefit is taken into account, the annual costs included on individuals in all programmes - supported, protected and subsidised - are lower than the average transferable disability benefits. Despite a number of positive effects, we would like to draw attention to our concerns, which should be examined and, if necessary, properly incorporated into the text of the law in the second reading. The bill introduces a series of innovations in the area of rehabilitation, approaches in particular individual treatment and choice of services. However, we need more emphasis on the importance and purpose of involving individuals and individuals primarily in the regular working environment and in the sense of self-employment or the establishment of independent enterprises and crafts, since this would undoubtedly further accelerate the employment of disabled people. We also lack more emphasis in the bill on non-discrimination, both during the rehabilitation process itself and in the search for a job or employment, because we believe that reference to the general labour law is not enough to prevent this. In our opinion, the role and task of the Employment Office is not sufficiently noticeable in the law and could be more defined, for example in the search for central jobs and in communication with potential employers. We believe that only the integration of actors will produce positive results. We further believe that in the proposal for a law, employment rehabilitation may be too similar to medical rehabilitation, which in our view is not correct, since medical rehabilitation can only be part of the first or is not necessary to be introduced to an individual. It is also not clear from this point of view that the medical profession is so strongly represented throughout the rehabilitation process and also in the composition of the commissions. Given that sub-legislative acts are fundamental to the implementation of the law in the LDS, we believe that they would be well-known in the adoption phase of the law at least in the grave draft, but that it is a one-year deadline for their preparation. Experience with other laws has shown that too long a deadline for the preparation of submarital acts is not justified and sometimes even has an unproductive effect. The proposal for a law introducing quotas also brings bonuses and minorities to businesses, both in the private and public sectors. Since we would like the measure to have positive effects on the increase in the employment of persons with disabilities, it would also be good to have a more accurate assessment of the impact of the measure on the competitiveness of Slovenian companies, which is reflected in the range of quotas in a specific common European market and, of course, an accurate assessment of the level of resources to be collected in line with the promotion of the employment of persons with disabilities and on this basis an assessment of the financial implications of the proposal, as an adequate level of funding will be one of the key elements for achieving the objectives set. At the conclusion of this, my respectable colleagues and colleagues, allow me to remind you that today there are some 600 million disabled people in the world. Each of us can become disabled at any time. The fact is that disabled people are both children, women and men worldwide deprived of basic human rights. They are often excluded from social life and discriminated against. Therefore, for people with disabilities in Slovenia, the bill that we are considering today is a positive step towards improving the situation, new opportunities for social inclusion and full life, so we will also support it in the Liberal Democracy Club. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2004-02-25-SDZ3-Redna-34.ana.u140	Thank you very much. In particular, an explanation of the appropriate jobs and proposed regulation in the law. The appropriate jobs are not as we used to know, typical jobs, such as cleaning jobs, maintenance jobs that are suitable for disabled people, but are those jobs where each specific employer will look at in himself where a specific job could hire a disabled person, or a disabled person who has become disabled during the working relationship with that employer, or that he may employ another disabled person at some job and adapt this job to the appropriate communication, information technology and employment of a blind disabled person. The employer will focus on which jobs are suitable, where he can employ disabled persons who are able to work in that job and identify such jobs in his systemisation. This is also necessary because the Labour Relations Act allows persons with disabilities to be employed in certain jobs without invitation to tender and that in those jobs that will be defined as suitable in the systemisations, the employment of a disabled person without a job invitation will be possible. By regulating suitable jobs, we also follow the Disability Management Code adopted in 2001 by the ILO and travels, not binding, but just travelling to make sure that employers, concrete, each individual, start working on disability in specific jobs. And that is followed by this arrangement. Not by doing, for example, one list of typical jobs where people with disabilities would be employed. The problem in the law is that people with disabilities are also employed in higher jobs, not just on service services, because also disabled people are able to do so if they have an adapted working environment or a job for their disability needs. We have experienced quite a number of problems in disabled companies in recent years, but we were unable to influence them in a timely manner because we did not have any grounds to look into certain documents and events in this company. The problem of restricting or retaining the impact of employment in disabled companies is also the fact that people with disabilities are often employed in old-age enterprises who become disabled in a parent undertaking, but at the same time there is no other appropriate work in a disabled company, which should actually be. It also seemed to us that September 15th was the shortest period that we, as a legislator, should be made available to the commercial industry to adapt to the new legal situation, as well as the minimum period within which the government would have to draw up sub-legislative acts. This law will incorporate two important solutions and only in a qualitative manner. The first solution is to identify what essential life products are or are essential to life. This category is already somewhat confused by the modern concept of life and work, but it was brought through a referendum in our space and the legislator found some approximation with that category that is in the profession called FNCG, that is, fast turning consumer goods. Another issue, which is merely qualitatively defined in the law, is the criterion for identifying trades that will be treated as trades for essential necessities of life. This is quite a task for the government to draw up appropriate sub-legislative acts. We fear, however, that, with such a law as we are about to adopt, we are opening very well-founded questions about constitutional solutions primarily from the perspective of the free economic initiative and some other constitutional categories. As stated in the Parliamentary Group of the Liberal Democracy of Slovenia, we will not oppose the adoption of the harmonisation amendments.

ParlaMint-SI_2004-02-25-SDZ3-Redna-34.ana.u160	Mr President, thank you very much for your words. Dear colleagues and colleagues! Following the decisions of the National Assembly in the second reading of the law, we received a legislative summary by adopting amendments from the SDS to Articles 11 and 12a of the amended proposal, which continues to prevent a single policy in the field of awarding distinctions. The adoption of these amendments, however, allowed the applicants to submit amendments that eliminate disputed solutions in the law and allow the uniform management of the policy of decorations in the Republic of Slovenia. We would therefore like to make an amendment to Article 11 so that the award of the "honorary mark of the freedom of the Republic of Slovenia" can continue to be awarded as the highest credit for the exclusive act of independence for the other credits related to the exceptional merits and actions of special importance for the welfare of Slovenia, but order and medals will be awarded in accordance with the law on the emoluments of the Republic of Slovenia. Therefore, the law of distinction of the Republic of Slovenia remains an honour mark in force and allows applicants who have not proposed for more than 10 years an award for acts of independence committed to do so to continue or, which is unlikely, to continue to apply the law if they had to defend the freedom of the Republic of Slovenia again. We have also tabled an amendment to Article 12a, which is linked to the amendments to Article 11. The proposed amendment, however, removes the duality of the criterion for the award of the honour mark or medal, which has so far been the main reason why the law on the decorations of the Republic of Slovenia has not been applied since 25.01.1995. So it wasn't a medal or a medal or any order. The confirmation that there was an obvious duality in the award of awards, namely only the case, on the basis of the third indent of Article 3 of the law on the embellishment of the "honorable mark of freedom of the Republic of Slovenia", the candidate could receive this award, and I quote: "for personal courage and self-sacrifice in the rescue and protection of human lives and material goods". On the basis of Article 6, paragraph 7, of the law on the decorations of the Republic of Slovenia, the candidate could receive a medal for courage, quote: "for personal courage and self-sacrifice in the salvation of human lives and material goods". The same text made it possible to give a sign of honor or a medal of courage. Then find out who can. Therefore, we propose deleting this indent in the law on the embellishment of the "honoral mark of the Republic of Slovenia", including the deletion of the fourth indent of Article 3 for the award of the honour mark of the Republic of Slovenia for the merits of establishing and strengthening international relations and the transfer of this area into the law on the decorations of the Republic of Slovenia and the statute of honours of the Republic of Slovenia brings European practice to this field. Thus, according to certain criteria, it will now be possible to award medals or medals on the basis of the rank of the diplomat who comes to us because they have similar things arranged in other laws. The deletion of the fifth indent of Article 3 of the Act on the Distinguishing of the Honorary Mark of Freedom of the Republic of Slovenia has helped to reduce the inflation of the assigned signs, since the merit line in other areas of life and work, which are important for the sovereignty and freedom of the Republic of Slovenia, has made it possible to provide an extensive field of distribution and thus to infuse these honorable signs. However, this field is precisely made in the Act of Medals, because in this area we have an appropriate order or medal in terms of the candidate's work, such as culture, economy, science, social, politics, diplomacy, the military, international organisation and so on. That is why we ask Members and Members to support the amendment and the law and thus enable the Republic of Slovenia to have a single policy in the field of awarding decorations. I would also like to inform you that, just before the third reading of the draft law, we have noticed that a technical error has occurred in the text of the statute of distinctions adopted in the second reading as an annex to the draft law.
